PREFACE 
  I, the Chairman of the Department-related Parliamentary Standing Committee on Home Affairs, 
having been authorised by the Committee to subm it the report on its behalf, do hereby present this 
One Hundred and Forty-Fourth Report on Demands for Grants (2010-2011) relating to the Ministry of 
Home Affairs (MHA). 
2.0 While examining the Demands for Grants each year, it has been the practice in this Committee 
to select one subject/issue for focussed study. “Disaster Management”, “Delhi Police and its 
functioning” and “Union Territories without Legislature” were some of the subjects examined by the 
Committee in the past. 
2.1  As part of the detailed examination of ‘Internal Security in the country’, the present Committee 
has decided to take up the following components thereof for in-depth examination: 
(a)  Naxalism; 
(b)  Cross-border Terrorism; and  
(c) Infiltration across the Borders; 
2.2 The Committee has also decided to focus on the following subjects: 
 (i)  UTs of Andaman and Nicobar Islands, Dadra and Nagar Haveli and Daman and Diu; 
 (ii)  Census 2011 and National Population Register; and 
 (iii)  Arms, Fire Arms and Ammunition. 
2.3  The previous Committee had studied the subject “Housing Schemes for Central Police Forces 
and Delhi Police” but could not conclude it. This Committee has concluded the study and a chapter 
has been included in this report. 
2.4 The Committee has thus completed its study of all the aforementioned subjects. However, on 
‘Cross-border terrorism’, ‘Infiltration across the borders’ and ‘Arms, Fire Arms and Ammunition’, the 
Committee will present separate reports. 
3.0 The Committee held in all four sittings to co mplete the examination of Demands for Grants of 
the Ministry of Home Affairs. The Committee he ard the Home Secretary and other officers, on 
Demands of the Ministry of Home Affairs and other subjects selected for focussed study, in its 
meetings held on 22 and 23 March, 2010. The Home Secretary and his team of officers responded to 
the questions/queries of Members pertaining to the budgetary allocations of the UTs of A & N Islands, 
Dadra and Nagar Haveli and Daman and Diu, in the sittings of the Committee held on 5 and 12 April 
2010. In its sitting held on 12 April, the Committee also examined the subjects ‘Census 2011’ and 
‘National Population Register’. 
4.  The Committee while making its observations/recommendations, has mainly relied upon the 
following documents: 
(i) Address by the President of India to both H ouses of Parliament assembled together on 22 
February 2010;  
(ii) Speech of Finance Minister on 26 February 2010 while presenting the Union Budget 
2010-2011; 

(iii)  Implementation of Budget Announcements 2009-2010; 
(iv)  Detailed Demands for Grants of the Mini stry of Home Affairs for the year 2010-2011; 
(v) Annual Report of the Ministry of Home Affairs for the year 2009-2011;  
(vi) Outcome Budget of the Mi nistry for the year 2010-11; 
(vii) Detailed Explanatory Note on Demands for Grants of the Ministry of Home Affairs for 
the year 2010-2011; 
(viii)  Physical and financial targets fixed and achieved during the Tenth Plan period and for the 
year 2009-2010; 
(ix) Projection of outlays for the schemes to be undertaken by the Ministry during the 
Eleventh Five Year Plan; 
(x)  Details of under-utilization of the allocations made under different heads during the last 
three years; 
(xi)  Written replies furnished by the Ministry to the Questionnaires sent to them by the 
Secretariat;  
(xii)  Presentations made by the Home Secretary and other concerned officers; and 
(xiii)  Written clarifications furnished by the Ministry, on the points/issues raised by the 
Members during the deliberations of the Committee. 
5. The Committee considered this report in its sittings held on 22 and 26 April and adopted the 
same on 26 April, 2010. 
6. For facility of reference and convenience, observations and recommendations of the Committee 
have been printed in bold letters in the body of the Report. 
 
 
 
  M. VENKAIAH NAIDU  
NEW DELHI; Chairman, 
26th April, 2010 Department-related Parliamentary 
  Standing Committee on Home Affairs 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 

REPORT 
CHAPTER-I 
OVERVIEW 
1.1   According to the Entries 1 and 2 of List II - ‘State List’ - in the Seventh Schedule to the 
Constitution of India, ‘public order’ and ‘police’ are the responsibilities of States. Article 355 of the 
Constitution enjoins on the Union to protect every State against external aggression and internal 
disturbance and to ensure that the Government of every State is carried on in accordance with the 
provisions of the Constitution. In pursuance of these obligations, the Ministry of Home Affairs 
continuously monitors the situation, issues appropriate advisories, extends manpower and financial 
support, guidance and expe rtise to the State Governments for maintenance of security, peace and 
harmony without encroaching upon the constitutional rights of the States. 
1.2  The major responsibilities of the Ministry of Home Affairs are maintenance of internal security, 
management of para-military forces, border management, Centre-State relations, administration of 
Union Territories, disaster management etc. In addition, the Ministry also has the following broad 
responsibilities: 
(i) Issue of Notification in respect of Appointment of Pr esident/Vice-President, Prime 
Minister and Members of the Union Council of Ministers; 
(ii)  Appointment of Governors and Lt. Governors; 
(iii)  Regulation of Pay and Allowances of the Ministers; 
(iv)  Carrying out Census in India once every ten years; 
(v)  Implementation of the provisions of the Official Language Act, 1963; 
(vi)  Maintenance of communal harmony and promotion of national integration;  
(vii)  Co-ordination of policy relating to human rights; 
(viii)  Providing relief and rehabilitation to refugees/displaced persons; 
(ix)  Regulation of entry of foreigners into India; 
(x)  Grant of pension and other benefits to Freedom Fighters; and 
(xi)  Prevention and combat of drug abuse and drug trafficking. 
1.3   Under the Government of India (Allocation of Business) Rules, 1961, the Ministry of Home 
Affairs has the following constituent Departments: 
(i) Department of Internal Security; 
(ii) Department of States; 
(iii) Department of Home; 
(iv) Department of Jammu & Kashmir (J&K) Affairs; 
(v) Department of Border Management; and 

(vi) Department of Official Language. 
1.4  The Department of Official Language has a separate Secretary and functions independently. The 
Departments of Internal security, States, Home, Jammu & Kashmir Affairs and Border Management 
function under the Union Home Secretary and are inter-linked. 
1.5  The Ministry is further divided into following eighteen divisions for administrative purposes:  
(i)  Centre State Division 
(ii)  Internal Security Division 
(iii)  Police Division 
(iv)  Union Territories Division 
(v)  North-Eastern Division 
(vi)  Jammu & Kashmir Division 
(vii)  Foreigners Division 
(viii)  Freedom Fighters & Rehabilitation Division 
(ix)  Human Rights Division 
 (x)  Disaster Management Division 
 (xi)  Border Management Division 
(xii)  Police Modernization Division 
(xiii)  Policy Planning Division 
(xiv) Finance Division 
(xv)  Judicial Division 
(xvi)  Administration Division 
(xvii) Coordination Division 
(xviii) Naxal Management Division 
1.6  The Ministry has the following Central Police Organisations functioning under its 
administrative control: 
 (i) Assam Rifles; 
 (ii) Border Security Force; 
 (iii) Central Industrial Security Force;  
 (iv) Central Reserve Police Force;  
 (v) Indo-Tibetan Border Police;  
 (vi) National Security Guard; and  
 (vii) Sashastra Seema Bal. 

1.7  Delhi Police also functions under the administrative control of Ministry of Home Affairs. 
1.8  The following are the other institutions/organisations functioning under the administrative 
control of the Ministry: 
(i)  Bureau of Police Research and Development; 
(ii)  National Crime Records Bureau; 
(iii)  Central Finger Print Bureau; 
(iv)  Directorate of Forensic Science; 
(v)  LNJP National Institute of Criminology and Forensic Sciences;  
(vi)  Central Forensic Science Laboratory (CFSL); 
(vii)  Directorate of Coordination (Police Wireless); 
(viii) Narcotics Control Bureau; 
(ix) Bureau of Immigration; 
(x)  Central Detective Training School; 
(xi)  Central Hindi Training Institute; 
(xii)  Central Translation Bureau; 
(xiii) Chief Controller of Accounts, Ministry of Home Affairs;  
(xiv)  Committee of Parliament on Official Language; 
(xv)  Home Guards; 
(xvi)  Intelligence Bureau; 
(xvii) Inter-State Council Secretariat; 
(xviii)  National Civil Defence College; 
(xix)  National Emergency Response Force and Civil Defence;  
(xx)  National Fire Service College; 
(xxi)  National Foundation for Communal Harmony; 
(xxii)  National Human Rights Commission; 
(xxiii) North Eastern Police Academy; 
(xxiv)Registrar General, India; 
(xxv) Repatriates Cooperative Finance and Development Bank Ltd.;  
(xxvi) Sardar Vallabhbhai Patel National Police Academy; and 
(xxvii) Zonal Council Secretariat. 
Major Programmes/Schemes 
1.9   Major programmes/schemes implemented by the Ministry of Home Affairs are listed below: 

(i) Construction of fencing, roads, border outposts and flood- lighting along the international 
borders; 
(ii) Schemes for Reimbursement of Security Related Expenditure (SRE);  
(iii) Scheme for Modernization of State Police Forces (MPF); 
(iv) Scheme for Police Housing; 
(v) Modernisation of Pr ison Administration; 
(vi) Schemes for promoting the use of Official Language; 
(vii) Rehabilitation Schemes/Projects; 
(viii) Police Network (POLNET); 
(ix) National Disaster Management/Mitigation Programmes/Projects;  
(x) Modernisation of Fire  and Emergency Services; 
(xi) Revamping of Civil Defence set up in the country; 
(xii) Central Scheme for assistance to victims of terrorist and communal violence; 
(xiii) Scheme for grant of financial assistance to States for strengthening their enforcement 
capability to combat illicit trafficking in narcotic drugs and psychotropic substances; 
(xiv) Schemes under the Registrar General and Census Commissioner, India; and 
(xv) Strengthening of North Eastern Police Academy, Shillong. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 

CHAPTER-II 
OVERALL  ASSESSMENT OF DEMANDS FOR GRANTS 
2.0   The total Demands for Grants of the Ministry of Home Affairs for the year 2010-11 are Rs. 
42376.66 crore. The detailed Demands for Grants 2010-11 of the Ministry of Home Affairs were laid 
on the Table of Lok Sabha on 9th March 2010. 
2.1  Rs. 42376.66 crore has been allocated to the Ministry under following ten Demands: 
(i) Demand No. 51 - Ministry of Home Affairs; 
(ii) Demand No. 52 - Cabinet; 
(iii) Demand No. 53 - Police; 
(iv) Demand No. 54 - Other Expenditure of the Ministry of Home Affairs; 
(v)  Demand No. 55 - Transfers to UTs with Legislatures; 
(vi)  Demand No. 95 - Andaman and Nicobar Islands: 
(vii)  Demand No. 96 - Chandigarh; 
(viii)  Demand No. 97 - Dadra and Nagar Haveli; 
(ix)  Demand No. 98 - Daman and Diu; and 
(x)  Demand No. 99 - Lakshadweep. 
2.2   The Demands of the Ministry are divided into two volumes - Volume-I (Demand Nos. 51 -55) 
and Volume - II (Demand Nos. 95 - 99). Volume-II  is related to the Union Territories without 
legislatures. The Demands “Transfer to Union Territory Government with Legislatures” covers 
assistance to Union Territories of Puducherry and Government of NCT of Delhi in the form of grants 
and loans. Non-plan support to meet non-plan deficit is intended only Puducherry, as NCT of Delhi 
has a surplus balance from current revenue.

and Grant-wise distribution of BE 2010-11 in percentage terms are as follows:

2.6   The MHA is directly responsible for the budgetary allocations provided under three Demands 
for Grants viz. Grant No. 51 - Ministry of Home Affairs, Grant No. 53- Police; and Grant No. 54- 
Other Expenditure of MHA. Five Grants (95,96,97,98 and 99) relate to UTs (without legislature). The 
respective administrative authorities of those UT s are largely responsibl e for the execution of 
budgetary allocations provided to them. Similarly, the Ministry of Home Affairs is not actually 
involved with the allocations provided in Grants No. 52 Cabinet and 55- Transfer to UTs (with 
Legislature) since the concerned Ministries of the Government are responsible for scrutinizing and 
sanctions of schemes under their charge. In the succeeding paras, Demands for Grants (demand-wise) 
of the Ministry have been discussed in detail: 
 

Demand No. 51 - Ministry of Home Affairs 
2.7   The Demand mainly includes expenditure relating to the MHA Secretariat, Narcotics Control 
Bureau, various commissions under MHA, Registrar General of India (Census), Official Language, 
Intelligence Bureau, Bureau of Immigration, Civil Defence, Home Guards, Inter-State Council, 
National Human Rights Commission and Payment to State Governments for administration of Central 
Acts and Regulations, etc. The budgetary provision of the Demand for 2010-11 is given in the 
following table:–– 
(Rs. in crore) 
 Actuals BE RE BE 
 2008-2009 2009-2010 2009-2010 2010-2011 
Non-Plan 1099.87 1311.00 1583.73 2371.68 
Plan 22.05 303.40 226.90 911.71 
Total 1121.92 1614.40 1810.63 3283.39 
 
2.7.1 The Committee was informed that the increase of Rs. 272.73 crore under ‘Non-Plan’ in RE 
2009-10, as compared to BE 2009-10 was mainly due to additional requirement of funds under the 
head ‘Salaries’ on account of the implementation of 6th Pay Commission Report and schemes of 
Registrar General and Census Commissioner of India. 
2.7.2 It was explained that the increase in BE 2010-11 under ‘Plan’ and ‘Non Plan’ Head over RE 
2009-10, is mainly due to carrying out Population Census in 2010-11. 
Demand No. 52 - Cabinet 
2.8  The Demand includes budget provision for –– 
(i)  Salaries of Cabinet Ministers, Ministers of State and Deputy Ministers; 
(ii)  Expenditure on travel by Cabinet Minist ers, Ministers of State and Deputy Ministers; 
(iii) Sumptuary and other allowances of Ministers; 
(iv)  Expenditure on salaries, travel, o ffice expenses etc. of Cabinet Secretariat; 
(v) Expenditure on salaries, travel and office expenses of National Security Council 
Secretariat; 
(vi) Expenditure on salaries, travel and office expenses of Office of Principal Scientific 
Advisor; 
(vii)  Expenditure on Special Protection Group; 
(viii)  Expenditure on salaries, travel, office ex penses etc. of Prime Minister’s Office and 
National Advisory Council constituted for implementation of National Common 
Minimum Programme; and 
(ix) Expenditure on Government hospitality and entertainment of foreign State Guests; official 
entertainment arranged at Rashtrapati Bhawan on behalf of the Vice-President and Prime 

Minister; reception on National Days, investiture and presentation of credential 
ceremonies etc.

2.8.2 The Committee was informed that the decrease of Rs. 76.05 crore in RE-2009-10 over BE-
2009-10 was due to implementation of 6th Pay Commission Report.  
2.8.3 The Committee was also informed that the incr ease of Rs. 43.30 crore in BE 2010-11 over RE 
2009-10 is mainly under Arms & Ammunition, POL,  Minor Works, Major Wo rks, , Motor Vehicles 
and Machinery & Equipments under Special Protection Group (SPG). 
Demand No. 53 - Police 
2.9  The Demand relates to expenditure on CR PF, NSG, BSF, ITBP, CISF, Assam Rifles, SSB; 
Education, Training and Research; Criminal Invest igation and Vigilance; Inter-State Police Wireless 
Scheme; Special Police; Modernisation of Poli ce Forces; Delhi Police; NCRB; India Reserve 
Battalions; construction of residential accommodation and office buildings for police; Indo-
Bangladesh and Indo-Pak Border Works; Hi-tech Surveillance and creation of infrastructure for 
borders and coastal security; critical infrastructure  in extremism affected area; crime and criminal 
tracking network systems; assistance to counter insurgency and anti- terrorists schools; setting up of 
integrated check posts, etc. The budgetary provis ion of Demand No. 53 is given in the accompanying 
table:

2.9.1 The Committee was informed that the decrease  of Rs. 2889.95 crore under Non-Plan in RE 
2009-10 over BE 2009-10 was mainly under Salaries, Office Expenses, Other Administrative 
Expenses, Supplies and Materials, Arms & Ammunition, other charges (Voted). 
2.9.2 It was also informed that the decrease of Rs. 1070.39 crore under Non-Plan in BE 2010-11 over 
RE 2009-10 was mainly under Salaries and the decrease of 19.37 crore under Plan in RE 2009-10 over 
BE 2009-10 was on account of slower pace of ex penditure. The increase of  Rs. 110.06 crore under 
Plan in BE 2010-11 over RE 2009-10 was mainly due to higher provisions for North Eastern Police 
Academy, Central Forensic Science Laboratory and Delhi Police and for Residential Buildings of 
CPMFs, ‘Crime & Criminal Network Tracking System’ and ‘Setting up of Integrated Check-posts.’

rehabilitation measures for Kashmiri migrants, Modernisation of Prison Administration, National 
Disaster Management, Regional Institute of Correctional Administration, Chandigarh, National 
Identity Card Scheme, Civic Action Programme in North East, etc. The budgetary provision under the

2.11.1  Provision under this Demand is made for transfer of funds to Union Territories (with 
Legislature), i.e., NCT of Delhi and Puducherry for following purposes: 
(i)  Grant to meet non-plan deficit to UT of Puducherry; 
(ii)  For making payment of enhanced compensation for death, injury and damage to 
residential properties in Delhi during 1984 riots; 
(iii) Grants for Union Territories Plan Schemes; and 
(iv) Loans and Advances to UT of Puducherry to cover gap in resources under Non-Plan. 
2.11.2  When enquired about sharp decrease in BE 2010-11 over RE 2009-10 under Plan Head for 
Transfer to UT Governments from Rs.2699.87 crore to  Rs.1401.l0 crores, the Ministry has explained 
that the sharp decline in the Plan allocation in BE 2010-11 from RE 2009-10 is mainly due to less 
allocation of funds under Commonwealth Games Infrastructure for Delhi and no allocation for TRP 
for Puducherry in BE 2010-11.

CHAPTER-III 
SCHEMES, POLICIES AND PROGRAMMES OF THE MINISTRY 
3.1  Modernisation of State Police Force (MPF) 
3.1.1 The Committee was informed that the Ministry of Home Affairs is assisting the State 
Governments for modernization of State Police Forces. Under this scheme assistance is provided, 
inter-alia, for procurement of modern equipments for surveillance, communications, forensic science 
laboratories, etc., weaponry, vehicles, computerization, training infrastructure and for construction of 
Police infrastructure viz., Housing/Police Stations/Out Posts/Barracks, etc. 
3.1.2 The BE 2009-10 allocation under MPF Scheme was Rs.1250 crore. After earmarking 5% of the 
allocation i.e. Rs.62.50 crore as Contingency Reserve, th e remaining amount of Rs.1187.50 crore was 
allocated to various States. However, the BE 2009-10 allocation of Rs.1250 crore was reduced to 
Rs.1230 crore at RE stage. Out of this, Rs.108 0.58 crore was released to various States, as on 
17.3.2010. 
3.1.3  Replying to the Committee’s query regarding the Management Information System (MIS) for 
proper monitoring and concurrent evaluation of the Scheme of Modernization of Police Forces in a 
comprehensive manner, the Ministry of Home Affairs in its written answer stated that no MIS has 
been developed for the assessment of the scheme. However, they had received a proposal from the 
Computer Maintenance Corporation (CMC) for the de velopment of the MIS on the lines of MISs for 
other Centrally Sponsored Programmes of the Government of India. 
3.1.4 The Ministry further stated that several States including Bihar, Chhattisgarh, Karnataka, 
Rajasthan, Maharashtra, West Bengal, Uttar Pradesh and Assam were yet to submit utilization 
certificates for 2007-08. The progress of utilization of MPF funds by State Governments was reviewed 
in Ministry of Home Affairs with representatives  of the State Governments from time to time. The 
State Governments had been advised to ensure speedy utilization of funds released by the Ministry of 
Home Affairs under the Scheme, through letters dated 21.10.2009 from Union Home Minister to 
Chief Ministers of States and again on 12th November, 2009 from Additional Secretary (CS) 
addressed to Chief Secretaries of defaulting States. The Ministry contended that after those requests, 
the States had improved their position barring the above mentioned States. 
3.1.5 The Committee was apprised that the Ministry was taking steps to ensure better compliance of 
implementation of the Scheme by non-performing States. The Ministry stated that as per Cabinet Note 
dated 14th January, 2009 approved by the Cabinet Committee on Security (CCS), 30th November 
shall be reckoned as the date upto which outstanding utilization certificates submitted by the State 
Governments shall be taken into consideration fo r release of MPF funds in a particular year. 
Thereafter, the unspent amounts retained from the cu rrent year’s allocation of the defaulting States 
shall be pooled up and released to States requiring more funds than their normal allocation on account 
of specific requirements and for better performing States with no pending/overdue utilization 
certificates. This decision penalises  the non-performing States and pushes them to utilize the funds 
released under MPF Scheme in the previous years expeditiously. 
3.1.6   Pertaining to the progress in terms of concurrent audit of the Scheme, to keep a watch on 
effective and timely utilization of funds, the Ministry of Home Affairs stated that a system of 

concurrent audit has been introduced in the Minist ry of Home Affairs, eff ective from March, 2009. 
Action Taken Reports furnished by the State Governments on the observations made in the 
Concurrent Audit Report were taken into consideration while making releases under the Scheme in the 
financial year 2009-10. 
3.1.7 It was also stated that the Concurrent Audit of MPF funds became a quarterly feature and audit 
for the first, second and third quarters of the financial year 2009-10 had been conducted. The audit 
reports of the first quarter had been sent to concerned State Governments seeking Action Taken 
Reports. ATRs from the State Governments were awaited. 
3.1.8 Regarding the outcome of laying more focus on the component of operational buildings such as 
police stations, police outposts and police lines as a part of effective budgeting for Annual Action Plan 
of States, under the modernization of Police Forces, during the financial year 2009-10, the Committee 
was informed that during the financial year 2009-10, the State Governments were advised to lay thrust 
on construction of police station buildings, police outposts and police lines in their Action Plan 
proposals under the Scheme and States were permitted to provide up to a maximum of 50% of the 
Plan size of the State for construction. Based on the Action Plans approved by the High Powered 
Committee in MHA, necessary funds had been released directly to the State Police construction 
agencies. 
3.1.9 The Ministry of Home Affair s further informed the Committee that  in addition, in the financial 
year 2009-10, 51 naxal-affected districts in eight States, namely Andhra Pradesh, Bihar, Chhattisgarh, 
Jharkhand, Maharashtra, Orissa, Uttar Pradesh and West Bengal were identified by MHA for grant of 
100% Central assistance under the MPF Scheme at the rate of Rs.2.00 crore per district. This Central 
assistance was meant for construction of police stations, police outposts and fortification of police 
stations/outposts in the naxal affected districts. It was stated that as the construction activities have a 
long gestation period, the impact/outcome of implementation of the action plan during 2009-10 would 
be known after a reasonable gap of time. 
3.1.10  When the Committee sought to know the details of impact assessment of the MPF Scheme 
done by the BPR&D and the findings thereof, the Ministry stated that BPR&D was entrusted with the 
job of undertaking a holistic study the impact of the Scheme in States and also to assess the 
requirements of the State Police Forces for the next 5-10 years. The study was outsourced to a private 
company, namely, M/s Ernst & Young, Gurgaon, and it has already signed an agreement with 
BPR&D and the study has commenced in January, 2010. 
3.1.11 The Committee is unhappy to note that the re is no Management Information System 
(MIS) for proper monitoring and concurrent evaluation of the Scheme of Modernisation of 
Police Forces. Now that the CMC has offered to de velop the software on the lines of MISs done 
for the other Centrally sponsored programmes of th e Government of India, the Ministry should 
take immediate steps to firm up the project so that a scientific monitoring mechanism of the 
scheme is in place at the earliest. 
3.1.12  The Committee is of the considered view that  the Scheme is of utmost importance so far 
as the maintenance of law and order in the States is concerned. Th erefore, the earmarked 
provision for the Scheme must be utilized fully  and optimally. In this connection, active 
participation of State Governme nts is necessary. The Committee, therefore, impresses upon the 

State Governments that Utilisation Certificates may be submitted in time so that they could 
avail of the funds under the Scheme. The Committee takes a serious view of the fact that even 
some of the States affected by left wing extremism are not properly availing the Scheme. Such 
States have not submitted the UCs for 2007-08 which would obviously deprive them in getting 
their full share of funds in future. The Committee fi nds it disturbing that on one side, the States 
are reeling under the impact of left wing extrem ism, as shown by the recent example of the 
Dantewada incident on 6.4.2010, in which a la rge number of security personnel were butchered 
by the ultras. On the other hand, the affected Stat es display lack of interest in availing of the 
Scheme. The Committee desires that the Minister of Home Affairs should discuss the matter 
with all the concerned Chief Ministers. 
3.1.13  The Committee appreciates introduction of the system of Concurrent Audit to monitor 
timely and appropriate use of the funds sanc tioned and released under the MPF Scheme. The 
Committee recommends that the Ministry of Home Affairs should apprise it of the highlights as 
well as the corrective steps contem plated in the light of the Action Taken Reports received from 
the State Governments. 
3.1.14  The Committee hopes that the report on impa ct assessment of the Scheme might have 
been submitted by now, as inte nded by the Ministry. If so, the Ministry should prepare action 
plan on the findings. 
3.1.15  The Committee, while appreciating the layi ng of more focus on the components of 
operational buildings such as po lice stations, police outposts and police lines as a part of 
effective budgeting for Annual Acti on Plan of States under the Modernization of Police Forces, 
feels that earmarking Rs.2.00 crore per district for naxal affected distri cts, is meagre. Keeping 
in view the use of latest weapons by the outlaws and their stra tegy to attack police 
establishments, the allocation and facilities to police establishments and personnel should be 
appropriately increased. However,  this should not be done across the board and should be 
linked to utilization. 
3.1.16  The Committee feels that in addition to providing the State and Centre Police Forces 
modern arms, equipment, surveillance devices, better communications facilities, etc., it is 
equally important to provide them  basic and specialized training . The Government should also 
not only train them for better interaction and communication skills with the public; but also 
raise their discipline, attitude, behavior, respo nsibility and accountability quotient, motivation 
and morale levels. 
3.2  Civil Defence 
3.2.1 The Ministry of Home Affairs stated that based on the recommendation of the GoM, an 
Approach Paper on National Policy on Civil Defence was prepared by a Committee under the 
Chairmanship of Shri K.M. Singh, Member, NDMA. Recommendations of the Committee were 
discussed in the meeting of the Union Home Minister’s Civil Defence Advisory Committee held on 
April 2, 2008. In the meeting, it was decided that the Civil Defence Act, 1968 may be amended to 
cater to the needs of disaster management so as to utilize the services of Civil Defence volunteers 
effectively for enhancement of public participation in disaster management related activities in the 
country. 

3.2.2  The Ministry also stated that as a follow up action, a Centrally Sponsored Scheme, with an 
outlay of Rs.100 crore, was launched in 2009 for revamping Civil Defence set up in the country 
during the Eleventh Five Year Plan. The Committee was also informed that with the aim to give a 
statutory back-up to the role of Civil Defence organization in disaster management, the Civil Defence 
(Amendment) Bill, 2009 was passed by Parliament in December, 2009. 
3.2.3 The Committee was also informed that the Scheme of Revamping of Civil Defence, has a pilot 
project involving an expenditure of Rs. 3.25 cr ore, focusing on the training of Civil Defence 
volunteers in the following areas:–– 
(i) Intelligence gathering, maintenance of communal harmony, prevention of rumour 
mongering, reporting of suspicious activities and maintaining general vigil in the area of 
their operation; 
(ii) Assisting Police in law and order situations, etc; 
(iii) Rescue and relief operation during manmade disasters; and  
(iv) Evacuation of casualties and providing first aid. 
3.2.4 There are two centrally sponsored schemes being implemented by the Directorate of Civil 
Defence, namely, (i) Revamping of Civil Defence set up in the country at a cost of Rs. 100 crore and 
(ii) Strengthening of Fire and Emergency Services at a cost of Rs. 200 crore. The Budgetary 
Allocation for both the schemes, during 2009-10 and 2010-11, are as under:–– 
(Rs. in crores) 
Name of the Scheme 2009-10 2010-11  
 BE RE BE RE 
Revamping of Civil Defence 
set up in the

 Country
