2.0  The Chairman welcomed the Members and officers to the meeting of the Committee. He 
apprised the Members about the agenda of the mee ting and asked the Secretary, Ministry of DoNER 
to make presentation on the Demands for Grants (2010-11) of the Ministry. 
3.0  Thereafter, a Presentation was made by the Secretary (DoNER). The Chairman then enquired 
about the Special Accelerated Road Development Programme for North Eastern Region (SARDP-
NE). He expressed his dissatisfaction over the fact that though the project was approved in 2005 it was 
still to take off. 

3.1  The Chairman then asked the Secretary to submit information on the posting and tenure of 
officers working in the Ministry an d NEC during the last five year s. He also observed that there 
should be some incentive for the officers who are posted in the North East so that they complete a 
certain mandated minimum period of tenure. 
3.2  The Chairman enquired about the Capacity Building/Technical Assistance and Livelihood 
Project where the targets were not achieved despit e huge plan increase from Rs.2.5 crore to Rs. 35 
crore in the Budget Estimates and the expenditure had been nominal. He took exception to last minute 
preparation of utilization certificates by differe nt agencies and observe d that the monitoring 
mechanism of the projects under the Ministry of DoNER was grossly inadequate. 
3.3  Regarding Non-Lapsable Central Pool of Resources (NLCPR), the Chairman took exception to 
the fact that only four projects, out of 258 infrastru cture projects had been completed in the last three 
years and there was delay in 143 projects. The Chairman also enquired about the status of 
implementation of Sarva Shiksha Abhiyan scheme in the North Eastern states. 
3.4  Clarifications were also sought by th e Chairman and Members on the following Issues: 
(i) North East Regi on Vision 2020; 
(ii) Hydro Electric Power Projects proposed by the State Government;  
(iii) Initiatives being taken to achieve the goal of investment of around rupees fourteen lakh 
crore in energy sector; 
(iv) Reasons for making no provision for Disaster Management System and Non-Conventional 
Energy Resources;  
(v) Unemployment problem in the region; 
(vi) Monitoring of expenditure of funds under various developmental projects and 
building/maintenance of roads under Pradahan Mantri Gram Sadak Yojana (PMGSY); 
(vii) Transport connectivity, empowerment of women groups, special provision for interest 
rebate to small entrepreneurs and other weaker sections for creating self-employment; 
(viii) Setting up of educational and health institutions in the NE region and tax rebates for them 
as was being given in Himachal Pradesh and Uttarakhand; 
(ix) Incentives to private sector for setting up industries in the region;  
(x) incentives given to cooperative societies for agricultural development; 
(xi) Alleged construction of airstrips on the border of Arunachal Pradesh by Chinese 
Government. 
  The Chairman then asked the Secretary, DoNER to send written replies to all the issues raised 
by him and the Members. 
  [Committee adjourned for lunch at 1.00 
P.M. and reassembled at 3.00 P.M.] 
4.0  The Chief Secretary, Andaman & Nicobar Is lands Administration made a presentation on 
Demands for Grants pertaining to that UT. 

4.1  The following issues were raised by th e Chairman and Members after the presentation: 
(i) Allocations for Indira Awas Yojana, Pradhan Mantri Gram Sadak Yojana, Rural 
Sanitation Programme, Drinking Water Programme, etc.; 
(ii) Expenditure on National Rural Health Mission, the Sarva Shiksha Abhiyan and the 
National Rural Employment Guarantee Act;  
(iii) Allocations for Old Age Pension Scheme; 
(iv) Lack of school buildings in rural areas and lack of facilities in the schools; 
(v) Enhancement of ceiling of per head expenditure under Mid-Day Meal Scheme and 
monitoring of quality standards of the meals supplied;  
(vi) Schemes for physically challenged persons, widows etc.; 
(vii)  Provision of stipends for students studying in mainland; 
(viii)  Steps taken to promote tourism in the region; 
(ix) Feasibility of setting up medical college in the islands; 
(x)  Issues related to rehabilitation of tsunami victims, fishermen etc.; 
(xi)  Steps taken to provide drinking water to the inhabitants; 
(xii)  Preservation of tribal culture; and 
(xiii)  Improving health facilities and reimbursement of expenditure for treatment in main land. 
4.2   The Chief Secretary, Andaman & Nicobar Islands  replied to the issues raised by the Chairman 
and Members. The Chairman asked him to send writt en replies to the queries not answered in the 
meeting. 
4.3  The Chairman then advised the Union Home Secretary to hold a review meeting with the 
officers of Administration of A&N Islands so as to take stock of all the issues raised in the meeting. 
5.0   The Chairman also advised the Planning Commission to look into the problems of erosion and 
shifting of agricultural lands and resulting problems of livelihood for the displaced people. 
6.0   The Committee then decided to postpone the discussion on Daman & Diu and Dadra & Nagar 
Haveli to its next meeting to be held on 12th April 2010. 
7.0  A verbatim record of the proceedings of the meeting was kept. 
8.0  The Committee then adjourned at 4.30 
P.M.

2.0  In the absence of the Chairman, Shri S.S. Ahluwalia was voted to the Chair.  
2.1  The Chair welcomed the Members to the sitting of the Committee and apprised them about the 
reference of the Code of Criminal Procedure (Amendment) Bill, 2010 to the Committee, and the 
following agenda for the meeting:- 
(i) Presentation to be made on Dadra & Nagar Haveli and Daman & Diu.  
(ii) Presentation on Census 2011 and National Population Register. 
[On arrival of the witnesses] 
Administration and Development of Dadra and Nagar Haveli and Daman and Diu 
2.2  The Chair welcomed the officials and asked the Home Secretary to make a presentation on 
Dadra and Nagar Haveli and Daman and Diu. 

2.3  The Administrator, Dadra and Nagar Haveli and Daman and Diu made presentations on the 
Administration and Development of the two UTs. 
2.4  After the presentations , the following issues were raised by the Members:--- 
(i) Inadequate subsidy given to poor families and wrong criteria for selection of BPL 
families. 
(ii) Regularisation of daily wages teachers in Dadra and Nagar Haveli. 
(iii) Death of two children beca use of infected food served in school under the Mid-day Meal 
Scheme and impact of Mid-day Meal Scheme on enrolment ratio in schools. 
(iv) Irregularities in purc hase of street lights. 
(v) Problem of drinking water in Randha-Dudhani in Dadra and Nagar Haveli.  
(vi) Issues regarding opening of colleges and hospitals in Dadra and Nagar Haveli.  
(vii)  Development of tourism, increase in produc tion of electricity, provision for kerosene to 
poor fishermen, prohibition of sewage influx in Damanganga river etc., in Daman and 
Diu. 
(viii)  Development of check dams, sewage pl ants, hospitals, engineering colleges, tourism 
development, airport and stoppage of Rajdhai and Garib Rath trains in Vapi etc. in Daman 
and Diu. 
(ix) Disturbing sex ratio in Dadra and Nagar Haveli and Daman and Diu. 
2.5  Some more queries regarding tele-medicine, tele-education, implementation of NREGA scheme, 
revenue collection of Dadra and Nagar Haveli, tax evasion in Dadra and Nagar Haveli and allocation 
to the UTs, were raised by some Members. 
2.6   Thereafter, the Chair sought clarifications on  the reported orders issued by the Home Secretary 
to the police chiefs of NCR region on surrender of Arms by holders of licensed Arms during 
Commonwealth Games 2010. He asked the Home Secretary to send a written reply on the same. 
  [The Committee then adjourned/or tea at 12.30 p.m. and reassembled at 12.50 p.m.] 
CENSUS 2011 AND NATIONAL POPULATION REGISTER 
3.0  The Chair then invited the Registrar General and Census Commissioner of India to make a 
presentation on Census, 2011 and the National Population Register. 
3.1  The Registrar General and Census Commissioner of India made a powerpoint presentation. 
After the presentation, the Chairman and the members of the Committee raised following issues and 
queries:--- 
z It would be difficult to hold back information contained in the Census Schedule: in view 
of the RTI Act, 2005. It is quite likely that even if exemption clause is invoked under RTI 
Act, 2005, the CIC, the Supreme Court or the High Courts may pass orders for disclosure 
of information. The Committee therefore recommended to Government to keep this aspect 
in mind. 

z The Ministry of Home Affairs maintained that the creation of National Population 
Register would be a comprehensive identity database in the country, for better targeting of 
the benefits and services under the Government Schemes/Programmes, improving 
planning and strengthening security of the country. Therefore, the ‘Nationality As 
Declared’ clause in the NPR Household Schedule has the potential to confer de facto  
Indian Citizenship to illegal migrants, despite the disclaimer attached to the clause. 
z The Government should not act in haste. There is a need to have a rethinking on the NPR 
exercise and the ‘Nationality’ clause should be removed as it has the potents in the long 
run which may pose a threat to the security a nd integrity of the country. Therefore, it may 
be worthwhile considering suspension of th e NPR exercise for the time being. Besides, 
the work relating to NPR preparation should be de-linked from the Census operations. 
3.2  The witnesses accordingly tried to clarify the i ssues raised by the Chairman and Members of the 
Committee 
4.  A verbatim record of the proceedings of the meeting was kept.  
5.  The Committee then adjourned at 1.50 
P.M.

  SECRETARIAT  
   Shri Tapan Chatterjee, Joint Secretary 
   Shri P.P.K. Ramacharyulu, Director 
   Shri D .K. Mishra, Joint Director 
   Shri Bhupendra Bhaskar, Assistant Director 
2.0  The Chairman of the Committee welcomed the Members to the meeting and informed them 
about the agenda for the day. He observed that the copies of the Draft 144th Report of the Committee 
on the Demands for Grants (2010-11) of the Ministry of Home Affairs had been received by the 
Members only in the morning and they did not have sufficient time to go through the same. In that 
context he explained that there was urgency in presentation of the Report to the Rajya Sabha and 
laying the same in the Lok Sabha before Guillotine was applied in the Lok Sabha on 27th April, 2010. 
He, therefore, suggested that Members could go thro ugh the draft report and if some changes were to 

be made, a meeting of the Committee could be held in the evening of 26th April, 2010 for adopting 
the same. 
3.0  At this stage the Chairman informed Members about the notification of the new Arms Policy. In 
that context he recapitulated that the Committee ha d already discussed the subject and made certain 
suggestions to the Ministry of Home Affairs for incorporating the same in the policy document. The 
Committee felt that the policy should be presented to Parliament first and then referred to the 
Committee so that a structured discussion could take place thereon. The Committee therefore decided 
to take up the Arms Policy separately and presen t a report thereon. It accordingly decided that the 
Chapter on Arms Policy may be taken out of the Draft Report. 
4.0  The Committee then took up for consideration the draft 144th Report. Members suggested a few 
amendments thereto. The Chairman directed the S ecretariat to incorporate those amendments in the 
draft and circulate the revised draft report amongst the Members. At this stage a Member pointed out 
that the Ministry of Home Affairs was yet to fu rnish responses to number of issues raised by the 
Committee. The Chairman asked the Secretariat to remind the Ministry of Home Affairs to expedite 
their responses so that those could be incorporated in the draft report. 
5.0  The Committee then decided to meet at 4.00 P.M. on 26th April for further consideration and 
adoption of the draft report. The Chairman observed  that if some Members wish to make suggestions 
for incorporation in the draft report, they may do so in writing to the Secretariat at the earliest. 
6.0  A verbatim record of the proceeding of the meeting was kept. 
7.0  The Committee then adjourned at 10.15 A.M. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 

XXI 
TWENTY  FIRST  MEETING 
  The Committee met at 4.00 P.M. on Monday, 26 April 2010 in Room No. 62, First Floor, 
Parliament House, New Delhi. 
  MEMBERS  PRESENT  
 1.  Shri M. Venkaiah Naidu  ––––      Chairman 
  RAJYA  SABHA 
 2.  Shri Rishang Keishing 
 3.  Shri S.S. Ahluwalia 
 4.  Dr. V. Maitreyan 
 5.  Shri Tariq Anwar 
 6.  Shri D. Raja 
  LOK  SABHA  
 7.  Dr. Rattan Singh Ajnala 
 8.  Shri Ramen Deka 
 9.  Shri Mohd. Asrarul Haque 
 10.  Shri Naveen Jindal 
 11.  Shri Lalubhai Babubhai Patel 
 12.  Shri Bishnu Pada Ray

 14.  Shri Hamdullah Sayeed 
 15.  Shri Ravneet Singh 
 16.  Shrimati Seema Upadhyay 
 17.  Shri Neeraj Shekhar 
  SECRETARIAT 
   Shri Tapan Chatterjee, Joint Secretary 
   Shri P.P .K. Ramacharyulu, Director 
   Shri D.K. Mishra, Joint Director 
   Shri Bhupendra Bhaskar, Assistant Director 
2.0  The Chairman welcomed the Members to the sitting of the Committee and apprised them of the 
agenda for the day. 
3.0  Before the Committee took up the listed agenda, a Member brought to the notice of the 
Committee the fact that the Ministry of Home Af fairs had since finalized the Arms and Ammunition 
Policy despite the subject-matter being under consideration of the Committee and that it had made a 
large number of suggestions to the Ministry. The Member expressed concern over the fact that the 
Policy had been released in the public domain and Parliament had not been informed about it even 
though it was in session. 

3.1  Responding to the issue raised by the Member, the Chairman of the Committee observed that 
the subject “Arms, Fire Arms and Ammunition” was very much within the jurisdiction of the 
Committee. He informed Members that the Secretar iat had told him that the Arms and Ammunition 
Policy was approved by the Home Minister on 5th April, 2010 and thereafter it had been placed on the 
website of the Home Ministry and that the same had not been laid in Parliament. The Chairman felt 
that the Committee could consider the Arms and Ammunition Policy, if it was referred to it by the 
Chairman or the Speaker, as the case may be, after the policy document had been presented to the 
Houses. He further observed that the Committee could also consider the Arms (Amendment) Bill after 
its introduction in either House and its reference to  the Committee by the Chairman or the Speaker, as 
the case may be. The Chairman then stated that before the Committee could decide on its future 
course of action, it would be necessary to examine the factual details with regard to the finalization of 
the Policy, its laying and implementation. 
4.0  The Committee thereafter took up for consider ation the draft One Hundred and Forty-fourth 
Report on the Demands for Grants (2010-11) of the Ministry of Home Affairs. After a brief 
discussion, the report was adopted with some changes. 
5.0  The Committee then decided to present the Report to the Rajya Sabha and lay it on the Table of 
the Lok Sabha on 27th April, 2010. 
5.1  The Committee authorized its Chairman and in his absence Dr. V. Maitreyan and Shri Rishang 
Keishing, Members, Rajya Sabha to present the Report in the Rajya Sabha. The Committee also 
authorized Shri Naveen Jindal and in his absence, Shri Bishnu Pada Ray, Members, Lok Sabha to lay 
the same on the Table of the Lok Sabha. 
6.0  The Committee then adjourned at 5.45 
P.M.
